For length 6121=1+3R, we can use a variable step process: at block j, choose a 4-term block with weights depending on j such that the sum distribution after R blocks is flat over 0..3R. This is exactly a nonhomogeneous Markov (adding increments 0..3 with varying distributions). We can choose distributions p_j(i) for each step j to make the cumulative sum distribution uniform. This is possible recursively by backward dynamic programming! We can design probability distributions p_j over {0,1,2,3} (or weights for a 4-term factor) such that the convolution of all p_j is uniform (or maximally flat) over [0,3R]. Since each factor is support length 4, the total polynomial length 1+3R. This is like factorizing a nearly flat polynomial into short factors. We can solve for p_j backward using the target flat distribution.

This is promising. We can construct A(x)=∏_{j=0}^{R-1} P_j(x), where each P_j is degree 3 nonnegative. The length is 1+3R if each factor has degree 3 and no gaps beyond? Product of degree3 polynomials yields degree3R. The coefficients are the sum distribution of R independent integer increments each in 0..3 with distributions P_j. We want A(x)^2 coefficients flat over 0..6R. But A(x) is already product of increments; A^2 is sum of 2R independent increments (the R from A and R from copy). Equivalently, the coefficients of A^2 are the distribution of sum of 2R variables, where variables are paired? Actually A^2 = ∏ P_j(x)^2. Each factor P_j(x)^2 is a distribution of sum of two independent copies of increment X_j, each in 0..3. The total sum is sum over j of Y_j, where Y_j = X_j+X_j' independent across j, with distribution Q_j = P_j*P_j supported 0..6. The length is 16R+1? Wait A^2 degree 6R, yes coefficients length 6R+1=2n-1 if n=3R+1? 2n-1=6R+1. Good. We have R blocks, each block's square contribution is a distribution Q_j over 0..6. The total A^2 is convolution over j of Q_j. We can choose P_j (and thus Q_j) to make the final distribution uniform over 0..6R. If we choose all Q_j identical with support 0..6, the sum distribution after R has support 0..6R and by CLT has width √R, not flat over length 6R (max ~1/√R, F~√R). We need nonstationary Q_j whose variances grow to fill the range? To get flat over 0..6R, each block should act like a "digit" with carries? Hmm. If Q_j are roughly uniform over 0..6, their sum CLT still width √R, not support 6R? Actually support max 6R, but probability concentrates near 3R with width ~√R * std(Q). The tails are very small; coefficients near edges ~0, not flat. To be flat over full support, increments must have large variance and be non-i.i.d? For uniform over 0..6R, each coefficient ~1/(6R). The distribution must have variance ~R? The support width is R, variance ~R. That's CLT width √R? Wait uniform over [0,6R] has variance (6R)^2/12=3R^2, which grows R^2, not R. A sum of independent bounded increments with total sum length O(R) has variance O(R), far smaller than uniform variance R^2. Thus a product of independent bounded increments (fixed support width) cannot be uniform over full length! The distribution of sum of independent variables with bounded increments is concentrated around mean with width √R, not spread to edges. To get mass at edges (sums near 0 or 6R), need variables that are often extreme, and positive correlation? But independent variables with bounded support: probability of extreme sum decays exponentially unless increments near extremes often. To be flat, need variance ~R^2, impossible for independent bounded with mean in middle; the maximum possible variance for bounded [0,6] is 9, so total variance ≤9R, std ≤3√R, while uniform std ~√3 R. So indeed convolution factors with bounded support per block cannot produce a flat distribution over the full support for large R. This explains powers being bad. Therefore we need arbitrary coefficients not factorable into independent bounded increments; i.e., correlations across positions (non-Markovian digits) are necessary to get flat over full length. The sequence a_i itself is not a product of independent small factors; its coefficients at different base positions can be correlated to make marginal sum X have support/fill all positions in a way that X+Y is flat.

But perhaps we can construct arbitrary sequence via "digital" correlations: assign weights to indices based on a low-discrepancy sequence (e.g., van der Corput) such that the distribution of X is "balanced" and X+Y flat. This is related to sum-free sets? Hmm.

Maybe the continuous flat-sum measure, if exists, must be singular with long-range correlations, not captured by small factors.

Given complexity, numerical optimization over all 6121 variables might be the only practical way. We need decide length.

For fixed f, maximize sum g subject to f*g ≤ M. This is a linear program. We can solve its dual with gradient/dual ascent:
Primal: maximize 1^T g, g≥0, T_f g ≤ M 1.
Dual: minimize M y^T 1, s.t. T_f^T y ≥ 1, y≥0.
Here T_f is n by (2n-1) matrix with (k,i)=f_{k-i} if valid? (full convolution coefficients). Since T_f nonnegative. The dual is finding a nonnegative measure y on convolution positions of minimum total mass such that for each i, (T_f^T y)_i = ∑_k y_k f_{k-i} ≥1. This is like placing weights on peaks to dominate f. We can solve dual via multiplicative weight update: y_k ← y_k * exp(η * violation?) If constraints <1 increase y. Objective ∑y. Starting y uniform? This may produce a dual solution. Complementary slackness: g_i>0 only where dual constraint tight. We can then get g as primal? Maybe simpler to use the dual y to propose a new f via previous iteration: if we have an optimal dual y for current f, the best g is? Since primal matrix is T_f; dual constraints active determine g. There is a relation: At optimum, g_i>0 only for constraints? We might not need full g; we can update f using y as gradient: to reduce future conv peaks? no_improve=0
```
while time.monotonic()<deadline:
   c,p = get_weights(a,beta)
   g = gradient(p,a)
   # Try a few learning rates: current lr, lr*0.5, lr*2
   improved=False
   for trial in [lr, lr*0.5, lr*2, lr*0.1]:
       cand = multiplicative_update(a,g,trial)
       sc = fast_score(cand)
       if sc + 1e-12 < a_score:
           a=cand; a_score=sc; improved=True; # maybe keep lr if trial == lr else set lr=trial
           break
   if improved:
       if score > best_score: update best
       no_improve=0
   else:
       no_improve+=1
       lr *=0.7
       if no_improve>20:
           # anneal: random perturb log a
           pert = np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.05,a.size))
           a = a*pert; normalize
           a_score=fast_score(a)
           beta *=1.5
           no_improve=0
   # Increase beta occasionally: if no_improve>10 beta*=1.1 up to 20000
   # If score very large, reduce beta?
```

```
def _fft_len(m):
    n=1
    while n<m: n*=2
    return n

def fft_square(a):
    n=a.size
    L=1 << (2*n-2).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:2*n-1]
    return c
```
For n=6121, 2n-2=12240, bit_length? L=16384. Good.

We can optimize by using `np.fft.rfft(p, L)` and `np.fft.rfft(a_rev, L)` each step.

Local search details more:
```
def local_search(a, deadline_frac, rng):
   a=normalize(a.copy())
   score=quick_score(a)
   best=a.copy(); best_score=score
   beta=50.0; lr=5.0; step=0
   while time.monotonic()<end:
       c = fft_square(a)
       M = c.max()
       # if M<=0 break
       z=c-M; p=np.exp(beta*z); p/=p.sum()
       g=grad(p,a)
       # ensure g finite, nonnegative?
       improved=False
       for mul in [1.0,0.5,2.0,0.1]:
           trial=lr*mul
           cand=a*np.exp(-trial*g)
           s=cand.sum()
           if s<=0 or not finite: continue
           cand/=s
           sc=quick_score(cand)
           if sc < score - 1e-15:
               a=cand; score=sc; improved=True
               if mul!=1.0: lr=max(1e-3,lr*mul)
               break
       if not improved:
           # reduce lr and try random perturbation sometimes
           lr*=0.8
           if step % 50 == 0:
              pert=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.05,a.size))
              a=a*pert; a=normalize(a); score=quick_score(a)
           beta = min(beta*1.1, 20000)
       if score < best_score:
           best=a.copy(); best_score=score
       step+=1
       # every 200 steps, print
   return best,best_score
```

Another exact improvement: For active max coefficient k, the constraint c_k ≤ M. If we fix M and maximize sum via LP, as in SOTA. We can approximate LP using a simple iterative: while time, solve dual via multiplicative weights and derive a new sequence `g`? Let's see if we can implement a primal heuristic for current f without heavy LP:
Given f normalized, M=conv(f,f).max. We want maximize sum g subject to conv(f,g)≤M. This is a linear program. We can solve it approximately using a greedy algorithm: Initialize g_i=0. Repeatedly increase the coordinate i with smallest marginal cost? Constraints are linear: each increase in g_i adds f_j to constraint k=i+j for j=0..n-1. We need keep all constraints ≤M. To maximize sum, allocate mass to variables until some constraints bind. This is like a continuous packing problem. We can use a water-filling / active-set: Start g=0, residual r_k=M. For each variable i, max possible increment limited by min residual over k where f_j>0 / f_j. To maximize sum, choose variable with smallest limiting coefficient? Since objective linear, optimal maybe Put all mass on a set of variables. Could use simplex but no.

But f one-sided sqrt has many residuals; maybe LP optimum g is just f? If f near optimal, no improvement. Not worth.

For α=0.5 continuous flat, but discrete corrections maybe below. Let's derive exact F for w_i=1/√(i+1). S=∑_{i=0}^{n-1} i^{-1/2} ~2√n + ζ(1/2)? The correction negative? M at c_{n-1}=∑_{i=0}^{n-1} 1/√((i+1)(n-i)). This is hypergeometric. There may be exact asymptotic M=π - (1/(2√n)) + ...? S^2=4n + const. F=π/2 + (something)/√n. What about w_i = 1/√(i) but with first term set to 1/√(i+c) with optimized c (shift). Let w_i=(i+c)^-α. Continuous still with shifted grid; may reduce peak. We can optimize c. The leading term ∫ (x+c)^-1/2 (k-y+c)^-1/2? As k large, shifts negligible, constant still π. So no.

Piecewise quantization may reduce leading constant? The leading term for any sequence approximating x^-1/2 on a Riemann sum should be π; quantization only corrections. To change leading constant, need continuous f different from x^-1/2. If x^-1/2 is minimizer of continuous problem with max π/4, no other smooth f can lower max (if theorem). Then finite improvements beyond O(1/n) impossible? Target 1.5027 = π/2 -0.068, O(1) not possible from continuous approximation. But discrete sequences can exploit corners not captured by continuous? For n large, any sequence can be viewed as a step function f_n with values a_i*n. If the continuous lower bound for all step functions is π/4, then inf over n is ≥π/2. Target would be impossible. Since target exists (given), either continuous bound is not π/4 or target for small n? Let's verify if f=x^-1/2 gives h=π/4 constant. Could there be an f with max h=1/2? Let's write helper functions carefully.

`next_power_of_two(m)`:
```
def next_power_of_two(m):
    if m <=1: return 1
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
```

`fft_convolve_real(a,b)`:
```
def fft_convolve_real(a,b):
    n=len(a)+len(b)-1
    L=next_power_of_two(n)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    fb=np.fft.rfft(b,L)
    out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,L)
    return out[:n]
```
For square, use same but one FFT:
```
def fft_square(a):
    n=len(a)
    L=next_power_of_two(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,L)
    return out[:2*n-1]
```
Potential negative tiny: `out[np.abs(out)<1e-12]=0`? Could clip `out = np.maximum(out,0.0)` for scoring? For gradient, c used for weights; negative zero not issue. Use `c = np.maximum(c,0.0)`.

`normalize_array(a)`:
```
a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float)
a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
s=a.sum()
if s<=1e-30:
   a=np.ones_like(a); s=1.0
return a/s
```
But if all zeros, uniform.

`quick_score(a)`:
```
a=np.asarray(a,float); if any negative? clip. s=a.sum(); if s<1e-3: return inf. a=a/s
n=a.size; if n==0 return inf
c=fft_square(a); c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
m=c.max()
return float(2*n*m)
```
Since normalized sum1. For exact evaluate, scale doesn't matter. Note if n=1, conv max1 ->score2.

`weights_power(c,q)`:
```
c=np.maximum(c,1e-300)
logc=np.log(c)
logc-=logc.max()
p=np.exp(q*logc)
tot=p.sum()
if tot<=0: p=np.ones_like(c)/len(c)
else p/=tot
return p
```
`weights_topk(c,k)`:
```
k=min(k,len(c))
if k==len(c): p=np.ones/c.size
else:
 idx=np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
 p=np.zeros_like(c); p[idx]=1.0/k
return p
```
`gradient_weights(p,a)`:
```
n=a.size
conv=fft_convolve_real(p,a[::-1])
g=2.0*conv[n-1:n-1+n]
return g
```
Check conv length = (2n-1)+n-1=3n-2. Segment valid.

`multiplicative_update(a,g,lr)`:
```
x=-lr*g
x=np.clip(x,-700.0,0.0)
new=a*np.exp(x)
s=new.sum()
if s<=0 or not finite: return a
new/=s
return new
```
If lr negative? no.

Local search:
```
def run_local_search(a, end_time, rng, verbose_label):
   a=normalize_array(a)
   score=quick_score(a)
   best=a.copy(); best_score=score
   lr=10.0
   step=0
   mode_cycle = [...]
   no_improve=0
   q_cycle_idx=0
   while time.monotonic()<end_time:
       mode=mode_cycle[q_cycle_idx%len(mode_cycle)]
       q_cycle_idx++
       c=fft_square(a); c=np.maximum(c,0)
       # If c max zero break
       if mode[0]=='top': p=weights_topk(c,mode[1])
       elif 'pow': p=weights_power(c,mode[1])
       elif 'lse': p=weights_lse(c,mode[1]) # maybe use power enough
       g=gradient_weights(p,a)
       # sanitize g: if inf set 0
       g=np.nan_to_num(g, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
       improved=False
       # try lr and 0.5*lr
       for mul in [1.0,0.5,2.0,0.25]:
           cand=multiplicative_update(a,g,lr*mul)
           sc=quick_score(cand)
           if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < score - 1e-12:
              a=cand; score=sc; improved=True
              if mul!=1.0: lr=max(1e-3,lr*mul)
              break
       if improved:
          no_improve=0
          if score < best_score - 1e-12:
             best=a.copy(); best_score=score
          lr=min(1000.0,lr*1.05)
       else:
          no_improve +=1
          lr=max(0.1,lr*0.8)
          if no_improve % 25 ==0:
             # random multiplicative perturbation
             scale=0.05 if no_improve<200 else 0.1
             pert=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, scale, a.size))
             a=normalize_array(a*pert)
             score=quick_score(a)
             if score < best_score: best=a.copy(); best_score=score
          # occasionally reset to best and perturb
          if no_improve > 200:
             a=normalize_array(best*np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.1,best.size)))
             score=quick_score(a)
             no_improve=0
       step+=1
       if step%200==0:
          print(f"{verbose_label} step {step} score {score:.6f} best {best_score:.6f}")
   if score < best_score: best=a.copy(); best_score=score
   return best, best_score
```
Need avoid infinite if `fft_square` for small arrays? fine.

Candidate generation:
```
def make_candidate(n, kind, params):
   i=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
   if kind=='pow': w=(i+1)**(-alpha)
   elif 'powshift': w=(i+c)**(-alpha)
   elif 'step': w=1/np.sqrt(np.floor(i)+1)
   elif 'uniform': w=np.ones(n)
   elif 'tilt': w=(i+1)**(-alpha)*np.exp(t*i/(n-1))
   elif 'tilt2': w=(i+1)**(-0.5)*(1+c*np.cos(np.pi*i/(n-1)))
   elif 'both': w=1/np.sqrt(i+1)+1/np.sqrt(n+1-i)
   return normalize_array(w)
```
Ensure positive; for cos may negative, clip.
`quick_score` handles.

Can we use a deterministic finite difference local search on a low-dimensional parameterization to guarantee finding below target? Maybe we can derive a sequence manually? Let's attempt further mathematical insights.

Suppose we want h(s) ≤ C. The Laplace transform F(p) should satisfy F(p)^2 ≤ C(1-e^{-2p})/p? For positive f? There is a method: Choose f(p)=sqrt(C(1-e^{-2p})/p) and invert. If inverse nonnegative, we have a solution. We can sample this function numerically via inverse Laplace using Fourier inversion? We can implement in code to generate a candidate! This could be powerful.

Laplace inversion: f(t)= (1/(2π i))∫ e^{pt} F(p) dp. For t∈[0,1]. We can approximate using inverse Fourier at p=σ+iω: f(t)=(e^{σ t}/(2π))∫_{-∞}^{∞} e^{-iω t} F(σ+iω) dω. We can choose σ large enough, sample ω grid, FFT. F(z)=sqrt(C(1-e^{-2z})/z). Need branch cuts; number negative? We can compute principal sqrt. Tilt candidate: `a = np.power(i+1.0, -0.5) * np.exp(tilt * i / max(1, L-1))`. If tilt positive, right endpoint huge; may be bad. Use both signs [-1,-0.5,0.5,1]. But if normalized, scale invariant; positive tilt increases right endpoint, might improve if one-sided should be decreasing? Our base decreases left high. Tilt positive makes right high; due reversal invariance, same as negative tilt with reversed sequence (score invariant). No need both? Reversal invariance means positive and negative equivalent; but base not symmetric, reverse of decreasing is increasing; scores same. Include one sign maybe -0.3 to left more? But left high already. Use [0.0,0.3,0.6] maybe not.

Cosine modulation: `a=np.power(i+1,-0.5)*(1+c*np.cos(np.pi*i/max(1,L-1)))`; clip. For c>0, right endpoint reduced (since cos π=-1), more left singular; maybe. Include c [0.1,0.3].

This adds candidates.

Let's include `both` endpoint: `a = 1/np.sqrt(i+1) + 1/np.sqrt(L+1-i)`. For i endpoints, values 1+...; may be high both endpoints, maybe bad, but test.

Now final exact evaluation of top candidates: If `evaluate_sequence` unavailable? It will be. If it returns np.inf due list type? We pass list floats.

Let's produce final code. Ensure no comments too long? fine.

Wait, one bug: In local search, `mode_cycle` contains `('pow', 0.0)`; weights_power with q=0: `logc = np.log(np.maximum(c, 1e-300)); logc -= logc.max(); p = np.exp(0.0*logc) -> np.exp(-0.0?) =1; OK. But if c contains zeros, log zero but exp(0) still1. Good.

In `weights_power`, if q large and logc very negative, exp underflows to0; p sum may still >0. If all underflow? max logc zero, at least one exp(0)=1. Good.

In `gradient_weights`, if p length 2n-1 and a length n, conv length 3n-2. `fft_convolve_real` uses next_power_of_two(n); for n maybe 18361 -> L=32768; segment `n-1:n-1+ a.size` correct. If conv has small imaginary? irfft real.

In `fft_square`, if a size 1, L=1? next_power_of_two(1)=1, rfft length1, works. But lengths >=8.

In `quick_score`, if n large and c max tiny, score finite. If c has negative max due to all negative? We clip to0. If a all zero, normalize to ones? `normalize_array` inside quick? We'll define `quick_score` to normalize:
```
def quick_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    if a.size==0: return inf
    a=np.maximum(np.nan_to_num(...),0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <=1e-30: return inf
    a=a/s
    c=fft_square(a)
    c=np.maximum(c,0)
    return 2*a.size*c.max()
```
If all zero, quick_score inf; add_candidate rejects. Good.

`normalize_array` separate.

Let's ensure `np.nan_to_num` parameters supported? Yes.

Now final.

One last thought: The initial parent `height_sequence_1` may have length 6121 and score 2, but it might be an optimized sequence with score 2? The target 1.5027 maybe from another. Our candidate generation includes parent; if parent has some structure, local from parent might find different. But we include only top candidates for local. If parent quick score 2 and candidates include sqrt 1.57, parent not selected. Good.

If height_sequence_1 is actually a very good sequence (score 1.5027) but previous program got 2 due bad perturbation? Wait "Length of construction: 6121. Target upper bound 1.5027. Your previous program achieved 2.000000." It might be that height_sequence_1 is the SOTA sequence with score 1.5027, but previous program returned uniform? No, previous program uses height_sequence_1 as parent and if it returns parent (no improvement) would score 1.5027, not 2. It achieved 2.000000, so height_sequence_1 likely score 2 (uniform). Target 1.5027 known externally. So parent not good.

We could use target value 1.5027 in local search? If best quick <1.5, we can still search. Not needed.

Let's final code. Ensure fenced python only.
</think>

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step functions minimizing
    2*n*max(convolve(a,a)) / sum(a)^2.

The program uses:
  - fast FFT-based score/gradient estimators,
  - a family of strong initial candidates (power-law, quantized, tilted),
  - multiplicative mirror-descent updates weighted by active convolution
    coefficients (top-k / power-mean weights),
  - random block/log perturbations to escape flat local minima,
  - exact evaluate_sequence() calls for final ranking.

All helpers are top-level; no lambdas are used.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import operator


def next_power_of_two(m: int) -> int:
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (int(m) - 1).bit_length()


def fft_convolve_real(a: np.ndarray, b: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Full real convolution via FFT."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    out_len = a.size + b.size - 1
    if out_len <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    L = next_power_of_two(out_len)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    fb = np.fft.rfft(b, L)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fb, L)
    return out[:out_len]


def fft_square(a: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Full convolution of a with itself, fast."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    out_len = 2 * n - 1
    L = next_power_of_two(out_len)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)
    return out[:out_len]


def normalize_array(a) -> np.ndarray:
    """Non-negative, finite, sum-normalized copy."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-30:
        # Invalid zero sequence: fallback to uniform.
        a = np.ones_like(a, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(a.size)
        if s <= 0:
            a = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def quick_score(a) -> float:
    """Fast normalized objective. Assume a is non-negative finite."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-30:
        return float("inf")
    a = a / s
    c = fft_square(a)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    return float(2.0 * a.size * m)


def add_candidate(cands, a, tag: str) -> None:
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size < 1:
        return
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-30:
        return
    a = a / s
    sc = quick_score(a)
    if np.isfinite(sc):
        cands.append((sc, a, tag))


def weights_topk(c: np.ndarray, k: int) -> np.ndarray:
    n = c.size
    k = int(min(max(1, k), n))
    p = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if k == n:
        p[:] = 1.0 / n
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
        p[idx] = 1.0 / k
    tot = p.sum()
    if tot <= 0.0:
        p[:] = 1.0 / n
    else:
        p /= tot
    return p


def weights_power(c: np.ndarray, q: float) -> np.ndarray:
    c = np.maximum(np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64), 1e-300)
    n = c.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if q == 0.0:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    logc = np.log(c)
    logc -= logc.max()
    p = np.exp(q * logc)
    tot = p.sum()
    if tot <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(tot):
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    return p / tot


def gradient_from_weights(p: np.ndarray, a: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    For an objective sum_k w_k c_k with c = a*a,
    gradient wrt a_i is 2 * sum_k w_k a_{k-i}.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    conv = fft_convolve_real(p, a[::-1])
    # Segment corresponding to i = 0..n-1.
    g = 2.0 * conv[n - 1:n - 1 + n]
    g = np.nan_to_num(g, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    return g


def multiplicative_update(a: np.ndarray, g: np.ndarray, lr: float) -> np.ndarray:
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    g = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)
    x = -float(lr) * g
    x = np.clip(x, -700.0, 0.0)
    newa = a * np.exp(x)
    s = float(newa.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-30:
        return a
    newa = newa / s
    return newa


def run_local_search(a0, end_time: float, rng: np.random.Generator, tag: str):
    a = normalize_array(a0)
    score = quick_score(a)
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        a = np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
        score = quick_score(a)

    best = a.copy()
    best_score = float(score)

    lr = 10.0
    no_improve = 0
    step = 0

    # Mode cycling: top-k exact max subgradients and power-mean smoothing.
    mode_cycle = [
        ("top", 1),
        ("pow", 1.0),
        ("top", 3),
        ("pow", 0.5),
        ("top", 5),
        ("pow", 2.0),
        ("top", 10),
        ("pow", 4.0),
        ("top", 50),
        ("pow", 8.0),
        ("top", 20),
        ("pow", 0.0),
    ]
    mode_idx = 0

    while time.monotonic() < end_time:
        c = fft_square(a)
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(c)):
            break

        mode = mode_cycle[mode_idx % len(mode_cycle)]
        mode_idx += 1
        if mode[0] == "top":
            p = weights_topk(c, int(mode[1]))
        else:
            p = weights_power(c, float(mode[1]))

        g = gradient_from_weights(p, a)

        improved = False
        # Short line search over learning-rate multipliers.
        for mul in (1.0, 0.5, 2.0, 0.25):
            trial_lr = max(0.01, lr * float(mul))
            cand = multiplicative_update(a, g, trial_lr)
            cand_score = quick_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(cand_score) and cand_score < score - 1e-14:
                a = cand
                score = cand_score
                improved = True
                if abs(mul - 1.0) > 1e-12:
                    lr = max(0.01, lr * float(mul))
                break

        if improved:
            no_improve = 0
            if score < best_score - 1e-14:
                best = a.copy()
                best_score = float(score)
            lr = min(1000.0, lr * 1.06)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            lr = max(0.05, lr * 0.82)

            # Random log-perturbation to escape stagnation.
            if no_improve > 0 and no_improve % 18 == 0:
                scale = 0.04 if no_improve < 200 else 0.09
                pert = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, scale, a.size))
                a = normalize_array(a * pert)
                score = quick_score(a)
                if score < best_score:
                    best = a.copy()
                    best_score = float(score)

            # If current state is far worse than best, reset from best with noise.
            if score > best_score * 1.35 + 1e-12 or (no_improve > 250 and rng.random() < 0.15):
                pert = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.08, best.size))
                a = normalize_array(best * pert)
                score = quick_score(a)
                no_improve = 0

        step += 1
        if step % 120 == 0:
            print(f"[local:{tag}] step={step} score={score:.6f} best={best_score:.6f} lr={lr:.3f}")

    if score < best_score:
        best = a.copy()
        best_score = float(score)
    return best, best_score


def make_candidate_from_params(L: int, kind: str, p1: float, p2: float) -> np.ndarray:
    n = int(L)
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    den = max(1, n - 1)

    if kind == "uniform":
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    if kind == "pow":
        return np.power(i + 1.0, -float(p1))

    if kind == "tilt":
        base = np.power(i + 1.0, -0.5)
        return base * np.exp(float(p2) * i / den)

    if kind == "cos":
        amod = 1.0 + float(p2) * np.cos(np.pi * i / den)
        amod = np.maximum(amod, 1e-6)
        return np.power(i + 1.0, -0.5) * amod

    if kind == "step_sqrt":
        # Quantized 1/sqrt(i) family.
        return 1.0 / np.sqrt(np.floor(i) + 1.0)

    if kind == "two_end":
        return 1.0 / np.sqrt(i + 1.0) + 1.0 / np.sqrt(n + 1.0 - i)

    if kind == "rev_pow":
        return np.power(n - i, -float(p1))

    # Fallback
    return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)


def propose_candidate(seed: int = 42, budget_s: float = 1500.0, **kwargs):
    """
    Returns a non-negative coefficient list.

    The function searches for a low-value sequence within budget_s seconds.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t_start + max(10.0, float(budget_s) - 35.0)

    # Determine main length from provided global if possible.
    main_len = 6121
    if "height_sequence_1" in globals():
        try:
            h0 = globals()["height_sequence_1"]
            if isinstance(h0, list) and len(h0) > 0:
                main_len = int(len(h0))
        except Exception:
            main_len = 6121
    if main_len < 8 or main_len > 20000:
        main_len = 6121

    # Candidate lengths: main length plus a few sizes.
    lengths = []

    def add_length(L: int) -> None:
        L = int(L)
        if 8 <= L <= 20000 and L not in lengths:
            lengths.append(L)

    add_length(main_len)
    add_length(main_len * 2)
    add_length(main_len // 2)
    for L in (8192, 4096, 2048, 1024, 512, 256, 128, 64, 32):
        add_length(L)
    if not lengths:
        add_length(main_len)

    # Generate a broad set of initial candidates.
    cands = []

    # Parent sequence, if present.
    if "height_sequence_1" in globals():
        add_candidate(cands, globals()["height_sequence_1"], "parent")

    alphas = (0.35, 0.42, 0.48, 0.50, 0.52)

    for L in lengths:
        # Core families.
        add_candidate(cands, make_candidate_from_params(L, "uniform", 0.0, 0.0), f"u{L}")
        add_candidate(cands, make_candidate_from_params(L, "step_sqrt", 0.0, 0.0), f"sq{L}")
        add_candidate(cands, make_candidate_from_params(L, "two_end", 0.0, 0.0), f"te{L}")
        add_candidate(cands, make_candidate_from_params(L, "rev_pow", 0.5, 0.0), f"rp{L}")

        for alpha in alphas:
            a = make_candidate_from_params(L, "pow", alpha, 0.0)
            add_candidate(cands, a, f"p{L}_{alpha:.2f}")

            if alpha == 0.5:
                for tilt in (-0.6, -0.3, 0.3, 0.6):
                    a = make_candidate_from_params(L, "tilt", 0.5, tilt)
                    add_candidate(cands, a, f"t{L}_{tilt:+.1f}")
                for cval in (0.08, 0.18, 0.30):
                    a = make_candidate_from_params(L, "cos", 0.5, cval)
                    add_candidate(cands, a, f"c{L}_{cval:.2f}")

    if not cands:
        a = np.ones(max(64, main_len), dtype=np.float64)
        return [float(x) for x in a]

    # Sort by quick score (score is first tuple element).
    cands.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))

    # Select local-search seeds.
    # Prefer: best overall, best at main length, second best overall if distinct.
    selected = []
    seen_tags = set()

    def select_candidate(idx: int) -> None:
        if idx < 0 or idx >= len(cands):
            return
        tag = cands[idx][2]
        if tag in seen_tags:
            return
        if cands[idx][1].size < 1:
            return
        selected.append(cands[idx])
        seen_tags.add(tag)

    # Best overall.
    select_candidate(0)

    # Best at main length.
    for i, item in enumerate(cands):
        if item[1].size == main_len:
            select_candidate(i)
            break

    # Next two distinct top candidates.
    for i, item in enumerate(cands):
        if len(selected) >= 4:
            break
        select_candidate(i)

    if not selected:
        selected = [cands[0]]

    # Keep a separate list of results for final exact ranking.
    results = []

    # Add initial top candidates directly.
    for item in cands[:40]:
        results.append((item[0], item[1], item[2] + "_init"))

    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 25.0:
        if selected[0][0] < 2.001:
            print(f"[start] best quick candidate: {selected[0][2]} n={selected[0][1].size} score={selected[0][0]:.6f}")

        # Allocate time to selected seeds.
        alloc = [0.52, 0.28, 0.15, 0.05]
        done = 0.0
        for idx_seed, seed_item in enumerate(selected):
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 20.0:
                break
            remaining_budget = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if idx_seed == len(selected) - 1:
                frac = 1.0
            else:
                frac = alloc[idx_seed] if idx_seed < len(alloc) else 0.1
            # Never reserve less than 20s for finalization.
            end = min(deadline - 5.0, time.monotonic() + frac * remaining_budget)
            if end <= time.monotonic() + 1.0:
                end = deadline - 5.0

            print(f"[search] starting seed {idx_seed}: {seed_item[2]} n={seed_item[1].size} score={seed_item[0]:.6f} end_lead={end - time.monotonic():.1f}s")
            best_seq, best_sc = run_local_search(seed_item[1], end, rng, f"{idx_seed}_{seed_item[2]}")
            if best_seq is not None and best_seq.size > 0:
                results.append((float(best_sc), best_seq, f"{seed_item[2]}_local"))

            done += 1

    # Finalization: evaluate top quick-score candidates exactly.
    results.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))

    final_best_seq = None
    final_best_score = float("inf")
    exact_limit = 25

    # Always try at least the first and a main-length candidate if possible.
    forced_idx = {0}
    for i, item in enumerate(results[:100]):
        if item[1].size == main_len:
            forced_idx.add(i)
            break

    evaluated = 0
    last = time.monotonic()
    for i, item in enumerate(results):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 5.0:
            break
        if evaluated >= exact_limit:
            break
        if i != 0 and i not in forced_idx and evaluated >= 8:
            # Avoid spending too much on marginal candidates.
            if item[0] > final_best_score and evaluated >= 5:
                continue

        seq = np.asarray(item[1], dtype=np.float64)
        if seq.size < 1:
            continue
        seq = np.nan_to_num(seq, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        seq = np.maximum(seq, 0.0)
        s = float(seq.sum())
        if s <= 1e-30:
            continue
        seq = seq / s

        try:
            exact = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in seq]))
        except Exception:
            exact = float("inf")

        evaluated += 1
        if np.isfinite(exact) and exact < final_best_score:
            final_best_score = exact
            final_best_seq = seq.copy()
            print(f"[exact] improved: tag={item[2]} n={seq.size} score={exact:.6f}")

        # Simple heuristic: if we already have a very strong value, stop earlier.
        if final_best_score < 1.35 and evaluated >= 10:
            break

    if final_best_seq is None:
        # Final guaranteed fallback: the best quick candidate, normalized.
        final_best_seq = np.asarray(results[0][1], dtype=np.float64)
        final_best_seq = np.maximum(np.nan_to_num(final_best_seq), 0.0)
        s = float(final_best_seq.sum())
        if s <= 1e-30:
            final_best_seq = np.ones(max(main_len, 64), dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            final_best_seq /= s

    # Optional: if there is still plenty of time and final score is not great,
    # do a short exact-guided polish around the final sequence.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 10.0 and final_best_score > 1.50:
        try:
            exact_now = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in final_best_seq]))
            if np.isfinite(exact_now):
                final_best_score = exact_now
        except Exception:
            pass

        # A few safe multiplicative-coordinate perturbations around final best.
        base = final_best_seq.copy()
        base_score = quick_score(base)
        for _ in range(200):
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 8.0:
                break
            L = base.size
            width = max(2, int(L // 200))
            start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, L - width)))
            stop = min(L, start + width)
            cand = base.copy()
            mult = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.08, size=stop - start))
            cand[start:stop] *= mult
            cand = normalize_array(cand)
            sc = quick_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < base_score - 1e-12:
                base = cand
                base_score = sc
                if base_score * 2.0 > 0:
                    pass
        if base.size > 0:
            final_best_seq = base
            try:
                ex = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in final_best_seq]))
                if np.isfinite(ex):
                    final_best_score = ex
            except Exception:
                pass

    final_best_seq = np.asarray(final_best_seq, dtype=np.float64)
    final_best_seq = np.nan_to_num(final_best_seq, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    final_best_seq = np.maximum(final_best_seq, 0.0)
    s = float(final_best_seq.sum())
    if s <= 1e-30:
        final_best_seq = np.ones(max(main_len, 64), dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        # Keep absolute scale modest; evaluation is scale-invariant.
        final_best_seq = final_best_seq / s

    print(f"[final] n={final_best_seq.size} best_score={final_best_score:.8f}")
    return [float(x) for x in final_best_seq]
```
